| C-169 | 3 | **ThreadPoolExecutor for prediction validation shares mutable state without synchronization.** `model.py:1380-1392` spawns a ThreadPoolExecutor to run `validate_and_save` across rolling-origin prediction sequences in parallel. The closure captures `configs` (a mutable `ConfigurationManager` dict), `self._model_path` (class-level mutable `_root`), and `self._save_predictions` (a bound method that calls `save_predictions()` which writes to disk). While individual DataFrames are independent per-iteration (each `raw_preds[i]`), the shared mutable objects (`configs`, `_model_path`) have no thread-safety guarantees. `ConfigurationManager.__setitem__` has no lock. In practice, no thread mutates `configs` during validation — `validate_and_save` only reads — but there is no structural guarantee preventing a future change from introducing a mutation. Additionally, `CorePredictionSniffer` constructs a new instance per call (safe), and `save_predictions_func` writes to different paths per `idx` (safe). The risk is latent: currently safe by coincidence (read-only access), not by design (no locking, no freeze, no documentation). Related: C-05 (ModelPathManager mutable _root — same class-level mutable state pattern), C-34 (_execute_model_evaluation complexity). | A developer adds per-sequence state mutation inside `validate_and_save` (e.g., accumulating validation statistics into a shared dict, or updating `configs` with per-origin metadata) — concurrent writes corrupt shared state with no diagnostic | repo-assimilation (2026-06-07) | Open |
| C-170 | 4 | **`_initialize_data_loader()` catches bare `except Exception` — masks programming bugs as missing queryset.** `model.py:897` catches `Exception` during `ViewsDataLoader` construction and sets `self._data_loader = None` with `logger.error("No Queryset detected for ViewsDataLoader. Skipping...")`. The intended case is a `KeyError` from `self.configs["steps"]` when no queryset is configured. But any programming error in `ViewsDataLoader.__init__` (e.g., renamed attribute, wrong parameter type, broken import) is also caught and produces the same misleading log. The model proceeds without a data loader, failing later in `_execute_data_fetching()` with a different, unhelpful error. Currently safe: `ViewsDataLoader.__init__` is assignment-only (no computation, no I/O, no validation). The risk is latent. Narrowing to `except (KeyError, ImportError)` would preserve the "no queryset" behavior while letting programming bugs propagate. Related: C-166 (Appwrite broad catch — same pattern class, different site), C-06 (dynamic script loading — Accepted). | A developer adds validation, computation, or I/O to `ViewsDataLoader.__init__` that raises an exception — the broad catch masks it as "No Queryset detected" and `_data_loader` is silently set to `None` instead of propagating the real error | tech-debt-cleanup (2026-06-07) | **Resolved (2026-08-04, #367) — and this entry's own mitigating claim was FALSE, which is why the tier was too low.** C-170 sat at Tier 4 on the grounds that *"`ViewsDataLoader.__init__` is assignment-only (no computation, no I/O, no validation)"*. It is not: it dereferences **three** attributes of its argument — `model_path.model_name`, `.data_raw`, `.data_processed` — any of which raises `AttributeError` on a malformed path manager. **Proven by the fix**: removing the swallow turned SIX green tests red in this repo. `test_model.py`'s fixture is `MagicMock(spec=ModelPathManager)` and `data_processed` is created at RUNTIME by `_initialize_directories`, so the spec never knew it existed — construction raised `AttributeError` on every one of those tests and the bare `except` hid it. Five tests had been asserting things about `execute_single_run` while the data loader silently failed to build; a sixth stubbed its config as `{}` and so raised `KeyError: 'steps'` every run. **The defect was concealing test failures in its own repository, which is the strongest evidence available that it would conceal production ones.** The message was also wrong on its face — an assignment-only-ish constructor cannot detect a queryset, yet reported *"No Queryset detected"* for ImportError, KeyError and TypeError alike, with `exc_info=False` discarding the traceback. And nothing handled the `None` it set: `model.py` calls `self._data_loader.get_feature_frame(...)` unguarded, so the failure relocated to an `AttributeError` in views-postprocessing and views-crafd. Now logs WITH the traceback and an honest message, then re-raises. Four tests pin it (`tests/test_managers/test_data_loader_construction_is_loud.py`), and the two stale mocks were completed rather than silenced. |

| C-173 | 4 | **Data-fetch WandB run creates a ghost entry in every model and sweep dashboard.** `_execute_data_fetching()` (`model.py:1139-1143`) creates its own WandB run with `job_type="fetch_data"` and `config={}` in the same project as the training/evaluation/sweep runs. | C-177 | 3 | **Module-level `dotenv.load_dotenv()` import side effect in `datastore.py` — RETIERED 4→3 (2026-07-30): now a named violation of a ratified platform contract with a scheduled removal.** PLATFORM-001 §3 clause 3 (þing-01, ratified 2026-07-28) forbids dotenv loading in importable/library code outright — "a library reading whatever `.env` the working directory holds is the disease this contract exists to cure" (S7). This single line is the mechanism the platform-wide þing formed around: it made one unowned laptop `.env` the de-facto origin of the seam's coordinates across six repos. Tier 3 (not 4) because the blast radius is cross-repo environment provenance, not local test hygiene. Deletion scheduled in #323, paired with registry-validated preflight. Related: C-11 (the preflight half), C-229 (the other silent-coordinate source). `modules/datastore/datastore.py:43` calls `dotenv.load_dotenv(dotenv.find_dotenv())` at module scope, so merely importing anything that transitively pulls in `datastore.py` triggers a filesystem traversal (`find_dotenv`) and mutates `os.environ` as an import side effect — independent of whether the datastore is ever used. This complicates test isolation (env state leaks across imports) and can surprise in headless/air-gapped/CI contexts where the `.env` discovered is not the intended one. Same pattern class as C-08 (PipelineConfig reads `pyproject.toml` at import, Accepted). No correctness impact — localized to environment/test hygiene. Related: C-08 (import-time side effect — same pattern). | When a test or tool imports a module that transitively imports `datastore.py` in an environment with an unexpected or absent `.env` — environment variables are mutated at import time with no opt-out **RESOLVED 2026-08-01 (#346, S6).** The module-scope `dotenv.load_dotenv(dotenv.find_dotenv())` is deleted from `modules/datastore/datastore.py`. **This is the line the platform þing formed around.** Verified by a subprocess probe that imports the module **from a directory containing a `.env`** and asserts `os.environ` is unchanged — running it in a clean directory would have passed even with the call present, so a second test loads the canary deliberately and asserts the detection fires. The probe can fail for the reason it exists. Two `dotenv` calls remain and are deliberately untouched: `model_path.py:371` and `dataloaders.py:931` both pass `dotenv_path=` explicitly, which is a caller naming a file rather than a library reading whatever directory its importer is standing in. **The deletion is not masked** — nothing else loads a `.env` at import, checked. Pinned by an AST guard, whose first version was itself too narrow: it banned `find_dotenv` only, while `load_dotenv()` with no arguments performs the same search. Caught by review — the third guard-scoping error of this epic (C-259, C-261) — and now the check bans the behaviour. **Operator-visible change:** anyone who relied on the ambient `.env` now gets a missing-variable error, so `PredictionStoreConfig.from_environment()`'s message says the auto-load is gone and what to do instead. | repo-assimilation (2026-06-18) | **Resolved** **Residual closed (2026-08-04, #377).** The #346 sweep left one survivor: `ModelPathManager.__init__` called `dotenv.load_dotenv(dotenv_path=self.dotenv)`. Not a *search* — a declared path — so the guard, which looks for `load_dotenv()` with no path, correctly did not flag it. But it still mutated `os.environ` PROCESS-WIDE as a side effect of constructing a path object, and this class is built by validation code and file utilities that want no such thing. It also made the repo's own error message untrue: `PredictionStoreConfig.from_environment` raises with *"pipeline-core no longer auto-loads a .env from the working directory"* while one constructor still did. The call is gone; the `self.dotenv` PATH attribute stays (nothing depended on the load — `from_environment` fails loud with the missing variable names, so removal cannot fail silently). New guard `test_no_constructor_loads_a_dotenv` bans `load_dotenv`/`find_dotenv` in ANY `__init__`, mutation-tested against two different constructors, and deliberately still permits the honest form — `dataloaders._get_viewser_update_config` loads a declared path inside the function that needs it and fails loud twice. |

| C-193 | 2 | **Producer-side cross-repo contract: pipeline-core's public `PredictionFrame` constructor is built by engine repos, and breaking it has no guard — the mirror of C-135 (consumer-side).** Engine repos construct PFs via `views_pipeline_core.data.prediction_frame.PredictionFrame` with the legacy `(y_pred, identifiers={time,unit})` signature: **views-hydranet** (`prediction_frame_assembler.py:182`) and **views-baseline** (`helpers.py:110`, `baseline.py:285`). #188's migration to the leaf constructor (`PredictionFrame(y_pred, SpatioTemporalIndex(time, unit, level))`, level required) is a breaking change to that public symbol — it broke hydranet eval at construction with a `TypeError`. Two governance gaps surfaced: (1) the break was first staged as pipeline-core **2.3.0** (minor); the engines' `views-pipeline-core <3.0.0` pin would NOT have gated it — corrected to **3.0.0** (major) in #206 so SemVer keeps un-migrated engines on 2.x, but **nothing enforces that a breaking change to a public symbol triggers a major bump** (recurs at #191's `ReconciliationModule` contract); (2) no cross-repo construction-contract test (C-30) catches producer breakage before release. Mitigation: major bump + lockstep engine migration (filed views-hydranet#137, views-baseline#21) + a leaf-side factory (views-frames#113, D-38). Related: C-135 (consumer-side mirror), C-187/C-188 (the migration), C-30 (no cross-repo contract test), C-48/C-165 (no abstractions), C-190 (boundary version floor). Cluster A.  **NARROWED (2026-07-31, review-rr strategic) — the incident half is history; the governance half is live.** The 3.0.0 major bump landed (`pyproject.toml` version = 3.0.0), the engine migrations were filed and the leaf-side factory question was settled (D-38). **Residual: nothing still enforces that a breaking change to a public symbol forces a major bump** — it remains a convention held by whoever notices, and the 3.0.0 release now in flight (#313) is precisely the event this entry says is unguarded. Cheapest durable fix: a public-symbol signature snapshot test, or a release checklist item in the #313 runbook that names the symbols engine repos construct. | When the next breaking change lands on a public symbol that engine repos construct/consume (a new required `PredictionFrame` field, a changed `ReconciliationModule` contract) and is staged as a minor bump — engine pins won't gate it, exactly as the 2.3.0-staged #188 break would not have | expert-code-review (2026-06-24, views-frames adoption) + hydranet #188 break report + review-rr strategic (2026-07-31) **UPDATE 2026-08-05 (#405): the enforcement gap is closed.** C-193 was consciously accepted for 3.0.0 with the guard unbuilt; `tests/test_public_surface_requires_a_major_bump.py` now builds it. It snapshots the **52 names the 12 subpackage `__init__.py` files already declare** via `__all__` and `_LAZY_EXPORTS` — discovered by parsing those files, never listed — together with the version the snapshot was taken at, and fails when the surface gets smaller or more demanding while the major stays put. **Scope was widened beyond what the plan asked for, and the reason matters:** the plan specified a snapshot of the 52 *names*, which would have watched #188 rename nothing and remove nothing and sail straight through — because `PredictionFrame` kept its name and changed its **constructor**. So the snapshot records each exported callable's parameter names, kinds and requiredness. Defaults and annotations are excluded deliberately: changing a default breaks no caller, and including them would generate the refactor-noise that gets guards disabled — the failure mode the plan itself warned about, and C-256's lesson. Breaking = a name disappears, a parameter disappears or is renamed or changes kind, or a required parameter appears. Adding a name or an optional parameter passes silently. Verified against the real package by reshaping an exported dataclass four ways: required-param-added at an unchanged major FAILS; the same change with the major bumped FAILS demanding a re-baseline (so the snapshot cannot rot behind the code); an optional param PASSES; the untouched tree PASSES. The comparison logic is additionally pinned by seven parametrised cases, because the live surface is correctly stable and those branches would otherwise never execute — #400's lesson that a guard whose logic never runs is not a guard. Does **not** cover: anything outside the declared surface, behavioural changes at a stable signature, or return types. Stated rather than implied. Related: C-135 (the consumer-side mirror), C-256, #374, #400. | Open (narrowed — bump done; enforcement gap outstanding, 3.0.0 release-gate item) |

| C-268 | 2 | **`eval_type="long"` requested 37 rolling-origin sequences while the enforced partition geometry supports 13 — silently reporting 12 of 36 steps.** Two authorities disagreed on the sequence count: `core_config_sniffer.py` derives it from geometry (`num_sequences = (test_end - base_origin - time_steps)//stride + 1` = 13) and *mandates* that geometry (`raise NotImplementedError` unless `test_len == time_steps + MAX_SHIFT_COUNT` = 36 + 12 = 48 months), while `_resolve_evaluation_sequence_number("long")` hardcoded `3*MAX_SHIFT_COUNT + 1` = 37. 37 sequences would need a 72-month window the sniffer rejects. The extra 24 sequences forecast past the actuals horizon, so `EvaluationAdapter`'s `actual.index.intersection(df.index)` matched progressively fewer months — down to 12 — and step-wise evaluation truncated to the shortest sequence. Verified against every real model config: **256 of 256** (model, run_type) combinations, uniformly `steps=36, window=48mo -> shortest_seq=12, 24 steps dropped`. Never invoked anywhere across the 18 `views-*` repos: every reference was a docstring, the CLI validator, this repo's own arg-parser tests, or a doc table. **Same failure shape as C-70 (Tier 1) on the same resolver** — an eval_type accepted by the CLI and asserted by tests but unsupported by the geometry; `complete` crashed loudly and was caught, `long` degraded quietly and was not. Note `live` returns `MAX_SHIFT_COUNT + 1`, identical to `standard`, so of four documented values one crashed, one truncated, and two were the same. Found while auditing a views-evaluation change against real caller paths (views-evaluation#44). Fixed in #378: `long` rejected at the CLI boundary and `_resolve_evaluation_sequence_number` raises `NotImplementedError`; `complete`'s message no longer recommends `long`. **Residual: the two-authorities split remains** — the count is still hardcoded per eval_type rather than derived from partition geometry, so a future value can diverge the same way. The assignment spans four lines and my pattern did not match it — the third time in this repo that a grep-shaped answer was wrong where reading the file would have been right. **The key was dead on both sides:** its own comment called it a shim for models using the pre-split convention, and **zero** model or ensemble configs define it — so it fired on every run and always produced exactly the concatenation. Two components disagreed about a key neither needed and no model supplied. Retired in #380: both synthesis sites deleted, 13 read sites now derive via `combined_targets(config)`, which also **raises** if a retired key reappears rather than preferring it as the old shim did. The equivalence was proven over six config shapes rather than assumed — identical in all, except that the old code crashed on `None` and the new one returns `[]`. pipeline-core's own model and ensemble templates also emitted the retired `metrics` key, so newly generated models would have hit the same wall; both corrected. **Root cause remains open as their C-33 and our C-262-adjacent concern:** we hand an evaluation-specific component our entire merged config, so our domain keys collide with names it has retired. Separating the configs is the real fix. | C-278 | 2 | **A manufactured zero was indistinguishable from an observed zero, and the scaffolding silenced the only signal that upstream provides.** `dataloaders.py:1255` calls `df = df.fillna(0.0)` in `_fetch_data_from_datafactory` **only** — the viewser and synthetic paths keep their NaN, and the FeatureFrame path deliberately does not fill (`feature_frame_path.py:19-21` says so). So the same missing observation becomes a hard zero or stays NaN depending on which fetch route ran, and nothing recorded that a substitution happened at all. The fill is mostly cosmetic — views-datafactory's zarr already zero-fills coverage gaps by design (their ADR-047: a filled month is *"structurally indistinguishable from months where the source observed zero events"*) — which is exactly why it is dangerous to do silently a second time downstream. views-datafactory ships one mitigation, a `UserWarning` from `load_dataset()`; **this repo suppressed it twice.** `templates/model/template_main.py` and `templates/ensemble/template_main.py` put a bare `warnings.filterwarnings("ignore")` into every generated model main, **Blast radius, corrected — the first version of this entry asserted "~130 scaffolded scripts per consuming repo" without counting, and it is wrong by two orders of magnitude.** Measured 2026-08-04: views-models' scaffolder does call this generator (`tools/scaffold/build_model_scaffold.py:201`), but of its 128 scaffolded mains (115 models, 13 ensembles) exactly **one** carries the blanket call — `models/fake_model/main.py`. The other 127 have no `warnings` import at all. So the suppression was waiting for future scaffolds, not sitting in a live fleet; the fix prevents recurrence rather than repairing damage, which is the *opposite* of #384, where 24 models had already regressed. This is C-273 again, in the entry that registers a C-273-shaped defect. And `pyproject.toml`'s pytest config listed `ignore::UserWarning`, so no test in this repo could assert on it either. Neither suppression was a decision about that warning — both were blanket settings that happened to cover it. This belongs to Cluster J but is **not** one of its three listed sub-shapes (failed read reported as absence; partial read reported as completeness; discarded result reported as success) — nothing here is discarded, and the read did not fail. It is a fourth shape: **a value the system never had, reported as a value it measured.** Recorded as such rather than filed under the nearest existing label, because a cluster that absorbs anything adjacent stops describing anything. Related: C-170, C-227, C-249, ADR-047 (views-datafactory). | When a model trained on datafactory input produces a forecast for a region whose source coverage lapsed, and the run is trusted because nothing warned | plan §4 / #366 (2026-08-04) | **Open (our half shipped 2026-08-04; the distinction does not exist upstream).** Three changes landed: the fill now logs a `logger.warning` naming the total, the model and the per-column breakdown before it substitutes; both templates narrowed to `DeprecationWarning` and `FutureWarning` by name; `ignore::UserWarning` removed from the pytest config. Guarded by `test_generated_main_does_not_blanket_suppress_warnings`, which parses the **generated** file rather than the template — the first version parsed the template module and found zero calls, because the template holds its output as a string literal, so it passed unconditionally. It was caught by mutation, not by review. **Then the replacement had the same defect one layer in**, found by the review round: it tested the call's *shape* (does it carry a `category=` keyword or a second positional argument) rather than its *effect*, so it accepted `filterwarnings("ignore", category=Warning)` — `Warning` being the base class of every warning, one identifier from the fix that shipped — and never looked at `simplefilter` at all. A third pass found two more conflations of *"narrow"* with *"unreadable"*: a non-literal action, and the bare `filterwarnings(...)` form from `from warnings import filterwarnings`. The guard now distinguishes four states (absent / literal / identifier / opaque) and reports the unreadable ones as unreadable. Mutation matrix: 15 blanket forms caught, 10 legitimate narrowings survive. The standing rule is now in ADR-056, per CLAUDE.md — it had been recorded only in a test. **That is the ninth guard here to be wrong about its own scope** (C-259, C-261, C-264, #346, C-277, the editable-tree check, C-207's figure, #400's dead `== "production"` branch at `views_pipeline_core/modules/validation/ensemble/check.py:159`, whose own error message says *"deployed"*) and the first written *after* that lesson was recorded. **The remaining half is not ours and is deliberately not being built:** distinguishing *"the source observed no conflict"* from *"nobody looked"* requires a schema that can express the difference, and the input data has no such field. ADR-047 line 68 says *"A future ADR may revisit this"* with no owner, no issue and no trigger. pipeline-core's position, filed upstream: we will not build gap-detection on a schema that cannot carry the distinction; when the input can express it, we will consume it. |

Fixed in #456/#457 by scanning sibling checkouts — but that fix works **only where checkouts are colocated**, so it is enforced on a developer's machine and never in CI. That is a standing limitation, not a defect to fix: no runner can see another repository. Same shape as the wire-fixture drift check and the SessionAuth registry ratchet. Related: C-288, C-218 (a suite structurally blind to the thing it was pointed at), C-59. | Onboarding a repository that consumes this package without also naming it here — or relying on the boundary check in CI, where the consumer half silently skips | falsification audit of the 3.1.0 release readiness claim (2026-08-13) | **Open (mitigated).** The sibling scan closes it for anyone with the platform checked out, which is whoever can act. Closing it properly needs a source of truth for who consumes this package that does not depend on either side remembering to mention the other. |
| C-290 | 1 | **An Extract Class refactor left a call to a method it had moved, and the resulting `AttributeError` was the only thing preventing a silent duplicate upload.** `file.py`'s `check_file_exists_by_hash` called `self._create_attribute_by_type(...)`, which has lived on `AppwriteProvisioner` since #331 and never on `AppwriteMetadataHandler`. Python resolves `self.X()` at runtime, so the module imported, linted and passed review; it raised only on the **first delivery to a metadata collection lacking `file_hash`** — once per partner, on the delivery that matters most. It killed the first CRAF'd delivery (views-crafdapi#45), reported as #473. **The dangerous part is what the crash was hiding.** Of three callers, only `_file_exists_by_hash` checked whether the lookup succeeded; `upload_file_with_metadata` and `upload_file_from_bytes_with_metadata` tested only for specific success codes, so a failure fell through to `upload_file(..., check_duplicates=False)`. Deleting the orphaned branch as #473 proposed — without touching the callers — would have converted a loud crash into a silent second copy: C-232's exact pathology at the two sites C-232's fix did not cover. Both callers were guarded **before** the branch was removed. Repair was rejected in favour of deletion for two reasons: it would reinstate create-on-read (C-233, ADR-046 §5), and it created `file_hash` at `size=255` where `FIXED_METADATA_ATTRIBUTES` declares 64 — a schema diverging silently per partner. Related: C-232, C-233 (whose Resolved status this corrects), C-227 (in-band failure discarded), C-218 (no double had ever been asked to raise this error string). | Any Extract Class or method relocation in this package — the language will not tell you, and neither will lint | views-models#473 (2026-08-14), investigated from three angles | **Resolved 2026-08-14 (#473).** Branch deleted; both callers guarded; `tests/test_no_orphaned_self_calls.py` derives per class that no `self.X()` resolves to nothing, across `views_pipeline_core/` and `tools/`. The hand-listed `not hasattr` guard that should have caught this had omitted the method. |
| C-291 | 2 | **`ensure-collection` reported OK while writing nothing, on the exact path an operator runs to repair a collection.** `main()` called `provisioner.ensure_collection()` with no arguments, so `metadata=None`; the existing-collection branch was guarded by `if metadata:` and skipped `ensure_attributes` entirely, returning `EXISTS` after two reads and zero writes. The creation branch passed `metadata or {}` and always ensured the schema, so the two halves of one method disagreed. An operator following `file.py`'s own instruction to run `ensure-collection` against a collection missing `file_hash` was told it was provisioned, and the next delivery failed identically. This is the C-227 shape inside the tool whose docstring states *"'OK' over a half-built schema is the same class of lie the þing convened about"*. **Compounding it, the command could not reach a partner collection at all**: `_ENV_MAP` hardcodes `APPWRITE_PROD_FORECASTS_COLLECTION_ID/_NAME` and the CLI exposed only `--bucket`, so `ensure-collection` always targeted `production_forecasts` whatever the operator meant. The method-level parameters existed and were unreachable. No test could see either: the two CLI tests patch `build_provisioner` to return a `MagicMock`, and no test exercised `ensure_collection()` with no metadata against an existing collection. | Adding a CLI action, or an `if <optional-arg>:` that gates a container-shaping write | views-models#473 (2026-08-14) | **Resolved 2026-08-14 (#473).** `--collection` / `--collection-name` added and threaded; a half-pair is refused in the shape of `audit/targets.py` (C-250); the existing-collection path now always ensures the declared schema; and the id/name match is `id`-anchored with a disagreement refused as `COORDINATE_MISMATCH` rather than resolved by listing order. |
| C-292 | 3 | **The provisioning CLI creates collections readable, writable and deletable by anyone, and locked-down buckets — undocumented, untested, and nowhere decided.** `provisioning.py` passes `Permission.{read,create,update,delete}(Role.any())` with `document_security=False` when creating a metadata collection. `Role.any()` appears at exactly one place in the repo and in no ADR, CIC, README or register entry; ADR-046's only mention of access control is a context sentence about Appwrite offering it. Meanwhile `ensure_bucket` defaults `permissions=[]` and `file_security=True`, and the CLI never overrides either — so one command produces a locked bucket and an open collection. No test inspects the argument. Provenance is the pre-þing code, relocated verbatim under #331's stated rule that a relocation must not change behaviour — so it has never been decided, only carried. Now more reachable, because #473 makes the CLI able to create partner collections. Deliberately **not** changed in #473: tightening permissions on partner-facing metadata is a decision with an external blast radius, and a hotfix unblocking a delivery is the wrong place for it. | Provisioning a collection for a new external partner, or any review of least privilege on the Appwrite key | #473 investigation (2026-08-14) | **Open.** Recorded rather than fixed. Needs a decision about what a partner metadata collection should permit, taken deliberately. |
| C-293 | 2 | **The register ranks by severity and nothing connects that ranking to work — the highest-tier entries are the least tracked.** Measured 2026-08-14 across the 28 open Tier 1/2 concerns: **11 cite no issue at all** (C-254, C-26, C-36, C-40, C-55, C-65, C-66, C-112, C-135, C-183, C-287) — there is no work item anywhere for them; **the sole open Tier 1, C-198, cites only issues that are now closed** (#233, #256); and 5 more are tracked solely by issues idle 40–74 days (C-206, C-186, C-187, C-191, C-197). **Concrete harm, not hypothetical:** C-186 is Tier 2 and this register calls it *Cluster A's FIRST OBSERVED-IN-PRODUCTION member* — runs OOM-killed at ~18 GB on a 32 GB box, root cause measured to a line. Both its issues (#181 the observation, #189 the durable fix) have had **zero comments and zero activity since June 2026** — 56 and 54 days — and the cited line is unchanged: `managers/prediction/prediction_frame_converter.py:74` still builds `[list(row) for row in pf.values]`, the list-in-cell construction named as the cause. Three releases shipped and two epics closed in that window. The register is reviewed for structure, deduplication and trigger quality (`/review-rr`); it has never been reviewed for whether its severity ordering corresponds to what is being worked on, and no test checks it. **This is not C-186** — that entry is the OOM; this is the absence of any mechanism linking tier to tracked work. Related: C-256 and C-259 (a record that was never reconciled with reality), C-273 (conclusions the evidence only makes consistent). | Preparing the next release gate, or a `/review-rr` pass that reports the register healthy without checking tracking coverage | Independent audit of our own governance artifacts (2026-08-14), derived from the register plus the GitHub issue index | **Open — tracked as #479.** No mechanism proposed yet; the measurement above is the baseline to improve on. |
| C-294 | 3 | **The god-class guard exempts by name and never re-checks the size of what it exempted, so exempted classes grow unbounded while it reads green.** `tests/test_falsification_no_god_classes.py` holds a 13-entry `KNOWN_GOD_CLASSES` allowlist. `test_no_new_god_class_appears` asserts `set(current) - KNOWN_GOD_CLASSES` is empty; `test_the_known_population_has_not_silently_shrunk` asserts the reverse. **Neither asserts anything about the size of an allowlisted class** — once a class is in the set, growth is invisible. Measured 2026-08-14 by AST against each entry's own `# NNN loc` comment: `ModelPathManager` 928→**1026** (+98), `ForecastingModelManager` 1630→**1681** (+51), `ViewsDataLoader` 913→**958** (+45), `AppWriteFileModule` 1534→**1576** (+42); three drifted down (ConfigurationManager −10, DataFrameEnsembleManager −22, PredictionFrameEnsembleManager −24). **7 of 13 comments are materially stale (>5 LOC); net +236 LOC of growth inside exempted god classes**, with the file passing 3/3 on the same day. The guard's docstring calls itself *a ratchet on the population* — it is a ratchet on **membership** only, and the LOC comments, the sole record of the sizes it froze, are decoration that nothing verifies. Same shape as C-256's un-reconciled worklist and C-259's hardcoded name set, both of which this file's own docstring cites as the reason it exists. Scope note: this concerns the guard, not the classes — those are C-36, C-44 and C-221. | Adding a class to `KNOWN_GOD_CLASSES`, or any refactor that grows a class already in it | Independent audit of our own governance artifacts (2026-08-14), AST measurement vs the allowlist's own comments | **Open — tracked as #477.** Cheapest fix is to make the allowlist a mapping of name → frozen LOC and fail on growth, which turns the comments into the assertion. |
| C-295 | 3 | **Dependency checking runs in one direction only — declared→installed, never imported→declared — so an undeclared runtime import is invisible to the whole suite.** `tests/test_declared_dependencies_match_reality.py` asserts every **declared** dependency's installed version satisfies its constraint, and that every declared dependency is actually checked. Nothing walks the import graph and asks the opposite question. Derived 2026-08-14 by AST-walking every module under `views_pipeline_core/` and diffing top-level imports against `[tool.poetry.dependencies]`, its groups and its extras: **13 third-party modules are imported in shipped code and declared nowhere** — pandas (21 sites), numpy (15), pyarrow (5), views_reporting (5), views_forecasts (4), tqdm (3), requests (2), views_frames_reconcile (2), toml, tabulate, datafactory_query, dotenv, yaml. Exactly **one** is deliberate and enforced — `views_reporting`, by ADR-054 and `tests/test_reporting_is_not_a_dependency.py`, and that guard hand-names the single package it is about. `datafactory_query` is a deliberate optional import with a loud `ImportError` and a version floor. The remaining eleven carry no recorded decision, so **nothing distinguishes *transitive on purpose* from *we forgot***. C-216 is the known instance and remains live. Note `pyarrow`: imported at 5 sites, undeclared, and `tests/fixtures/wire_contract/README.md` cites a pin `>=16.1.0,<17.0.0` that exists only upstream — the planned re-baseline is owned by #280 and is deliberately not duplicated here. | Any upstream dependency-resolution change, or adding a new import to shipped code | Independent audit of our own governance artifacts (2026-08-14), AST import graph vs pyproject | **Open — tracked as #478.** Cross-references C-216 (the one package); this entry is the missing guard. |

Hickey/Martin: the `run_type` guard encodes independent domain knowledge (ADR-018: freshness checks are conceptually forecasting-only) that should survive even if the `saved` invariant changes. Feathers: the guard IS tested in unit tests (`test_calibration_skips_freshness_checks`, `test_validation_skips_freshness_checks`) — it's not dead code, it's independently verified and would catch a regression if `args.py:411` is relaxed. | D-24 | **Should the `args.py:411` invariant be integration-tested?** Beck/Feathers: the three-site invariant (args.py + check.py + _create_model_args) is a Critical Business Rule that should be machine-verified. An integration test constructing `ForecastingModelArgs(train=False, saved=False)` and asserting it exits would codify the contract. Martin: the code is clear enough — `sys.exit()` at args.py:411 is self-documenting, and unit tests for check.py already cover the downstream behavior. Nygard sides with Beck — invariants that span module boundaries need explicit tests because no single module's tests cover the full chain. | D-27 | **ADR-054 "NEVER" — should it distinguish install-dependency from runtime-optional-use?** Martin (Clean Architecture): the NEVER rule correctly enforces the Dependency Rule — the inner layer must not know about the outer layer at any level. The 10 existing `from views_reporting` import sites are transitional shims, not permanent architecture. Weakening NEVER invites permanent coupling. Hickey (Simplicity): NEVER conflates two distinct concerns — (a) install-time dependency (pyproject.toml listing, circular resolution risk) and (b) runtime awareness (code that knows views-reporting exists). The codebase already violates (b) at 10 sites; pretending otherwise adds cognitive dissonance. A simpler framing: "pipeline-core's install footprint must not include views-reporting; pipeline-core may use views-reporting features when available at runtime, guarded by try/except ImportError." Feathers (Safe Change): amending a constitutional ADR is high-risk governance — the current NEVER provides a clear bright line. The 4 hard deferred imports work fine with runtime guards; adding an optional extra creates pressure to widen the coupling surface. Beck (Testing): CI already tests both states (with and without views-reporting). The architectural constraint is machine-verified regardless of how the ADR is worded. Related: C-144 (blocked by this question), ADR-054:80. | Martin+Feathers (keep NEVER absolute — bright line prevents erosion) vs Hickey+Beck (distinguish install from runtime — matches actual codebase reality) | Open |
| D-28 | **ReconciliationModule location: views-reporting vs views-post-processing vs pipeline-core.** Hickey: ReconciliationModule is mislocated in views-reporting — it's a core data operation (PGM-CM level reconciliation during forecasting), not presentation. Its only consumers are pipeline-core ensemble managers (`ensemble.py:747`, `dataframe_ensemble.py:921`). It should move to a semantically correct home. Feathers: it was just extracted via WET-before-DRY (ADR-054 PR 7). Re-relocating before the extraction stabilizes creates churn. Wait until shims are removed. Martin: the module violates SRP — views-reporting's responsibility is visualization and report generation, not data reconciliation. **User input (2026-06-06):** `views-post-processing` is the planned new home — semantically correct as post-inference data processing. This would separate it from both orchestration (pipeline-core) and presentation (views-reporting). The relocation should coincide with defining a typed I/O protocol for the reconciliation boundary (see C-167). **(2026-06-19):** that typed boundary is expected to be views-frames frame types, and the relocation must remove the cross-repo `reconciled_dataframe` mutation (C-182). Related: C-182 (cross-repo mutation), C-125 (Resolved — CRP heavyweight dep, deferred import mitigation), C-167 (no integration test on reconciliation schema), D-16 (Resolved — Fail Loud for configured reconciliation). | Hickey+Martin (relocate to views-post-processing — semantically correct, reduces false coupling) vs Feathers (defer — stabilize extraction first, relocate when shims are removed) | Open |
| D-29 | **Should ADR-055 mandate a pipeline-core boundary guard, or is per-repo enforcement sufficient?** Nygard/Beck: a contract without runtime detection at the boundary is documentation-only — silent violations produce the exact `big_chungus` incident the ADR was written to prevent. Martin/Ousterhout: pipeline-core should not know about model-internal scale; adding a scale-plausibility check re-introduces the coupling the ADR eliminates. Hickey: the `output_scale` key already violates the "no scale awareness in core" principle — the project chose safety over purity when it built `validate_output_scale_consistency()`. The existing infrastructure proves the precedent. | D-30 | **Should `output_scale: "log"` be treated as non-compliant, transitional, or a valid carve-out under ADR-055?** Kleppmann: Clause 1 (raw output) and Clause 3 (config is sole truth) are in semantic contradiction — `output_scale: "log"` is a config that truthfully declares non-raw output, making the config authority and the contract disagree. Hickey: the key's existence re-complects what the ADR claims to decomplect — if scale is model-internal, why does pipeline-core have `SUPPORTED_OUTPUT_SCALES`? Feathers/Nygard: `output_scale: "log"` is a useful transition mechanism — visible non-compliance is safer than invisible non-compliance; retiring it before all models return raw creates a detection gap. Related: C-174 (governance fragmentation), C-158 (ensemble scale consistency), D-25 (Resolved — template default, adjacent question). | Kleppmann+Hickey (semantic contradiction that must be resolved) vs Feathers+Nygard (transitional safety mechanism — keep until all models comply) | Open |
| D-31 | **Is prefix deprecation-without-removal coherent governance?** Hickey: `DatasetTransformationModule.ln_transform()` renames columns to `ln_*` — the code's behavior will always invite the semantic interpretation the ADR deprecates. A deprecation that changes no code and adds no warning is aspirational, not enforceable. GoF: the Strategy pattern works when the interface is clear; a "deprecated semantic interpretation" of an active code path is not a clear interface. Beck: where is the test that catches someone interpreting `ln_` as a scale signal? Without mechanical enforcement, the deprecation exists only in the ADR. Martin: deprecation is a recognized lifecycle stage; the CIC amendment and ADR cross-reference are the governance mechanism. The code change comes when `DatasetTransformationModule` is either retired or relocated to views-reporting (its canonical home per ADR-054). Related: C-174 (governance fragmentation — same ADR), Clause 5 of ADR-055. | Hickey+GoF+Beck (aspirational — not enforceable without code change or test) vs Martin (recognized lifecycle stage — CIC amendment is sufficient governance) | Open |
| D-16 | **Should `_load_c_dataset` / `_apply_reconciliation` fail loud or gracefully degrade when reconciliation IS configured?** Current contract: `_load_c_dataset()` returns `Optional[_CDataset]` (None on failure), `_apply_reconciliation()` treats None as non-fatal and returns unreconciled predictions. **Graceful camp** (Martin, GoF, Nygard): the method's role as a multi-tier fallback loader (provided → prediction store → local → None) is correct for operational resilience; network blips should not crash long-running ensemble runs. **Fail Loud camp** (Hickey, ADR-008, codebase philosophy): if reconciliation is *configured*, it is a Critical Business Requirement — silently publishing unreconciled predictions as reconciled is worse than crashing. The exception narrowing in C-71 accidentally moved toward Fail Loud (more exceptions propagate) but for the wrong reasons (incomplete handler, not a deliberate design choice). Related: C-68 (silent failure), C-71 (original narrowing), C-77 (incomplete narrowing). | Graceful degradation (return None, skip reconciliation, log warning) vs Fail Loud (raise PipelineException when configured reconciliation cannot complete) | **Resolved (2026-05-20):** Fail Loud. If reconciliation is configured, it is a Critical Business Requirement — crash with PipelineException rather than silently publish unreconciled predictions. C-68 and C-77 fixed accordingly. |

The check sits in the report stage, not `CoreConfigSniffer`, because the sniffer reads a config dict and cannot see the `-re` flag — it cannot distinguish a `dataframe` model that reports from one that does not, and only the first is deprecated. **It warns via `logger.warning`, not `warnings.warn(DeprecationWarning)`, and that is not a style choice:** ADR-056 has generated model mains silence `DeprecationWarning` by name, and reports run inside those mains, so a `DeprecationWarning` would be filtered out in the only process that emits it — the #366 defect, self-inflicted one layer down. | D-41 | **Retire the document store, or drill the exit first?** Kleppmann: the 14 database operations are the only real lock-in, and they are replaceable by the manifest mechanism ADR-013 already runs — *make the database unnecessary* and the exit collapses to 8 commodity storage calls. Nygard: you do not know your exit cost until you have paid it once; **drill first**, because a design chosen before the measurement is a guess. Both are cheap and neither blocks the other. | Kleppmann (data systems) vs Nygard (production reliability) | **Drill first — narrowly.** The drill is the only step that produces a number, and the number constrains the design rather than the reverse. It also costs 1–2 days against weeks for the retirement. But the tiebreak is thin: if the drill is deferred, do the content-addressed-id work anyway, because it pays for itself twice (C-254, and it deletes Cluster J's dedup path). Neither may precede C-241/C-249 — a Tier 1 that ships stale runs outranks a risk with no deadline. |

A check that skipped on both would stop checking the day they rename an attribute while still reporting green — the conflation this whole epic is about. That path is itself tested. A further guard AST-parses the package for any literal set/list/tuple containing their full vocabulary, so copying it back in fails rather than silently agreeing with itself; its first version grepped for the three strings and flagged prose in two files, which is how a guard gets switched off. Related: C-284 (the same verify-don't-duplicate move for the seam contract pin). **Two review findings, both fixed pre-merge.** (a) An empty-but-present vocabulary returned `set()` rather than raising: `getattr(..., None)` guards absence but not emptiness, so the contract test would have failed with *"we ask for X, they accept nothing"* — red, but describing the wrong problem. A husk is 'cannot verify', not an answer. (b) **The near-miss:** the no-copy guard watched sets, lists and tuples but not dicts — and our mapping IS a dict. `constants.py` already anticipates a future `feature_frame` entry, so a mapping written as dict values would have carried their entire vocabulary straight past the guard. | C-94 | 2 | **Timestamp source divergence between PF producer and PF ensemble consumer — ensemble can never find sub-model outputs.** `model.py:1294` (Track A+ eval) and `model.py:1481` (Track A+ forecast) save PredictionFrame outputs using `self.configs.get("timestamp", "")`, which is `datetime.now()` from `ConfigurationManager.__init__()` (configuration.py:182). `PredictionFrameEnsembleManager._evaluate_model_artifact()` (prediction_frame_ensemble.py:580) and `_forecast_model_artifact()` (prediction_frame_ensemble.py:621) construct load paths using `path_artifact.stem[-15:]` — the artifact creation timestamp embedded in the model filename. These are structurally different values: the artifact timestamp is from when the model was trained; the runtime timestamp is from when the sub-model evaluation/forecast runs. Consequence: `_load_or_generate_pf()` always misses the cache (path doesn't exist), triggers a subprocess, then fails to find the generated output (subprocess saved at runtime-ts path, ensemble looks at artifact-ts path), raising `PipelineException`. The entire PF ensemble evaluation and forecasting flow is non-functional. **Fixed (2026-05-22):** Replaced `configs.get("timestamp")` with `get_latest_model_artifact_path(run_type).stem[-15:]` at both Track A+ save sites. Producer and consumer now derive timestamp from the same artifact stem. Regression tests: `TestP2TimestampFromArtifact`, `TestP4ProducerConsumerPathAgreement`. Falsification tests: `TestP2TimestampDivergence`, `TestP4GenerateThenLoadBroken` in `tests/test_falsification_pf_ensemble_integration.py`. Related: C-16 (no integration tests — root cause of the gap). | When PredictionFrameEnsembleManager runs evaluation or forecasting against pre-trained sub-models — the first `_load_or_generate_pf()` call fails with PipelineException because generated output is at a different timestamp path than expected | falsify (2026-05-22) | Resolved |

| C-117 | 2 | **Extraction PR plans create circular pip install dependency.** PR 0 (`extraction_pr_plans.md:75`) declares views-reporting depends on pipeline-core (for `_ViewsDataset`, `ModelPathManager`). PR 9 (`extraction_pr_plans.md:972`) adds views-reporting as a hard dependency of pipeline-core (for re-export shims). This creates an unresolvable circular install: `pip install views-pipeline-core` requires views-reporting which requires pipeline-core. Neither `pip` nor `pip install -e` can resolve mutual hard dependencies without manual intervention. Resolution options: (1) make re-export shims use `try/except ImportError` so views-reporting is optional, (2) don't add views-reporting as a hard dep — shims are transitional and can fail gracefully, (3) use a single editable-install bootstrap script. Falsification test: `TestF1CircularDependencyResolution` in `tests/test_falsification_extraction_docs_sufficiency.py`. Ensemble managers (`ensemble.py:20`, `dataframe_ensemble.py:37`) are the only consumers. After extraction, importing `ReconciliationModule` from views-reporting transitively pulls in the entire views-reporting install (torch ~2 GB, scipy, geopandas, seaborn, plotly, matplotlib — ~2.5 GB total) for 298 LOC of optional reconciliation logic. This is the same heavyweight-dependency problem the extraction solves for pipeline-core, recreated in the other direction. The investigation notes reconciliation is "optional per-ensemble" (line 1508) but does not discuss the disproportionate dependency cost. Mitigation options: (1) keep reconciliation in pipeline-core (simplest — it's consumed only by pipeline-core code), (2) extract reconciliation to a third lightweight package, (3) make views-reporting's heavy deps lazy-loaded so importing reconciliation alone is cheap. Falsification test: `TestF2EnsembleDependencyProportionality` in `tests/test_falsification_extraction_docs_packaging.py`. Related: C-117 (Resolved — install-time cycle), C-47 (deferred imports). | Developer executes PR 7 as written — ensemble managers now import from views-reporting, pulling ~2.5 GB of heavyweight dependencies for 298 LOC of optional reconciliation | falsify (2026-05-27, extraction docs packaging principles audit) | Resolved — PR 7 now includes CRP note with deferred-import mitigation; investigation section 9 documents CRP concern (2026-05-27) |
| C-126 | 4 | **Re-export shims create undocumented runtime import cycle (ADP).** When views-reporting is installed, re-export shims create a bidirectional runtime import path: `views_pipeline_core.modules.statistics.__init__` → `views_reporting.statistics` → `views_pipeline_core.data.handlers`. Documents discuss the install-time cycle (PR 9 lines 1082-1100, "circular pip install") and prevent it via `try/except ImportError`, but never acknowledge the runtime import cycle. Python can resolve this because shims are leaf-level `__init__.py` files (no circular attribute access), but the cycle is fragile — import order matters and developers may encounter confusing `ImportError` or `AttributeError` during development. Falsification test: `TestF3RuntimeImportCycleAcknowledged` in `tests/test_falsification_extraction_docs_packaging.py`. Related: C-47 (deferred imports indicate cycles), C-117 (Resolved — install-time cycle prevented). | Developer working on views-reporting encounters intermittent ImportError when import order between pipeline-core and views-reporting changes (e.g., test discovery order, IDE auto-import) | falsify (2026-05-27, extraction docs packaging principles audit) | Resolved — PR 9 now documents runtime import cycle and resolution; investigation section 9 documents ADP assessment (2026-05-27) |

| C-132 | 2 | **ADR-054 shim pattern catches bare `except ImportError` — masks transitive dependency failures with misleading diagnosis.** The prescribed `try/except ImportError` re-export shim (ADR-054 lines 67-74) catches `ImportError` without chaining via `from e`. When `views_reporting` is installed but a transitive dependency (torch ~2 GB C extensions, scipy, geopandas) has a broken installation, ABI mismatch, or CUDA version conflict, the shim catches the *wrong* `ImportError` and tells the user "Install views-reporting" — a completely misleading diagnosis. The original traceback is silently discarded (no `from e`). Demonstrated with simulated broken torch: ADR-054 pattern produces `Original cause preserved? None`. The codebase's own precedent at `dataloaders.py:1171` already uses the correct `except ImportError as e: raise ... from e` pattern. All 9 shim examples (1 in ADR-054, 7 in extraction_pr_plans.md, 1 in investigation report) omit `from e`. Every extraction PR (1-8) will introduce shims with this defect unless the pattern is corrected before implementation begins. Falsification tests: `TestF1_NestedImportErrorMasking` (2 tests), `TestF3_MissingFromE` (1 test) in `tests/test_falsification_try_except_critical_infra.py`. | C-143 | 4 | **Ensemble managers import `ReconciliationModule` directly from `views_reporting` inside method bodies — late crash without views-reporting.** `ensemble.py:_reconcile_pg_with_c()` and `dataframe_ensemble.py:_reconcile_pg_with_c()` use deferred imports. If views-reporting is not installed and reconciliation is configured, the crash happens after sub-model forecasting completes. **Mitigated (2026-06-01):** PR #129 added `importlib.util.find_spec("views_reporting")` pre-flight check in `CoreConfigSniffer._check_reconciliation_config()` — import availability validated at config audit time before any compute. Related: C-125 (CRP concern — resolved), C-78 (pre-flight validation). | Pre-flight check catches missing views-reporting at config audit time. Residual: deferred import pattern still exists but crash window eliminated. | 2026-06-01 |

Execution pending — Resolved when views-models companion PR merges. | 2026-05-27 |

Also added integer truthy, None, fail-fast ordering, sweep characterization, and save arg assertion tests. CIC §10 updated. | 2026-05-26 |

Code has evolved to accept arbitrary target names: `evaluation/stage.py:93` uses `target_identifier = target` directly. Test `test_B1_non_standard_naming_allowed` proves it. | Dead `_get_eval_file_paths()` method removed; `generate_evaluation_report_name()` param renamed from `conflict_type` to `target_identifier`; misleading docstring fixed. Tech debt cleanup 2026-04-02. | 2026-04-02 |
| C-49 | 2 | **`ModelPathManager._build_absolute_directory` returned `None` on missing dirs with `validate=True`.** Logged a warning and silently assigned `None` to path attributes (`self.data_raw`, `self.reports`, ...), causing cryptic `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for /: 'NoneType' and 'str'` far from the root cause when downstream baseline/stepshifter managers did path arithmetic. Direct violation of the "Fail Loud and Proud" principle. | Option B (Diagnostic) applied: `_build_absolute_directory` now raises `FileNotFoundError` with a descriptive message when a path is missing and `validate=True`, matching the existing `_get_model_dir` pattern. Two permissive tests in `test_model_path.py` replaced with fail-loud assertions; `test_ensemble_path.py` fixture expanded to build a complete ensemble skeleton. 1092 tests green. | 2026-04-11 |
| C-46 | 2 | **No architectural boundary enforcement in CI.** Dependency direction (inner layers must not import outer layers) maintained by developer discipline only. Investigation (2026-04-11) showed the register description was partially stale: `tests/test_boundary_enforcement.py` already existed and enforced `data/`/`configs/`/`modules/` boundaries via AST parsing, but used file-level exemptions that hid real violations and did not scan `files/` at all. | Enforcement strengthened: boundary test now also scans `files/` (Layer 2) via new `TestUtilityLayerBoundaries` class; file-level exemptions for `modules/appwrite/file.py` and `modules/logging/logging.py` removed (both fixed by C-43 in same PR); remaining exemption narrowed from file-level to *granular symbol-level* — only `ModelManager`, `EnsembleManager`, `EnsemblePathManager` from `check.py` are allowed, and any additional manager import triggers a test failure. Runs in standard pytest suite via `run_pytest.yml` on every PR. Residual smell tracked as C-50. ADR-002 Enforced Boundaries section added. | 2026-04-11 |
| C-52 | 2 | **Drift detection silently lost during viewser→datafactory migration.** Drift detection is a viewser-specific feature (`Queryset.publish().fetch_with_drift_detection()`). Datafactory models have no equivalent. | Accepted: `_fetch_data_from_datafactory()` returns `None` for alerts. ViewsDataLoader CIC §11 documents the gap. Source-agnostic drift detection interface deferred until views-datafactory supports equivalent functionality. **UPDATE 2026-08-10 (#414, epic #410): the gap is now RECORDED, and still open.** Every cache artifact carries `drift_detection_ran` in its provenance record, derived from what the fetch actually returned — `alerts is None` means no detection channel, `[]` means it ran and found nothing. Those two were previously indistinguishable, which is this concern in miniature. Deliberately NOT derived from `source == "viewser"`: a hand-written source mapping goes wrong the day a source changes behaviour (C-259, C-261, C-264, C-282). **`False` does not block** — blocking would stop every datafactory model in the platform, which is most of the migration target — and the field is explicitly non-identifying, so it never causes a cache to be refused: a run about to READ a cache has not fetched and cannot know the value. **Stays open**: the upstream capability still does not exist. What changed is that a datafactory cache and a viewser cache are no longer indistinguishable on disk, so the missing safety net leaves a trace instead of an absence. | 2026-04-22 |
| C-69 | 1 | **`wandb.AlertLevel.WARNING` does not exist — runtime `AttributeError`.** `ensemble.py:634` used `wandb.AlertLevel.WARNING`. The wandb enum only has `INFO`, `WARN`, `ERROR`. This crashed at runtime when reconciliation returned `None`. Masked in tests by mocking wandb. | Fixed: `WARNING` → `WARN` in both `ensemble.py` and `dataframe_ensemble.py`. Test updated to not mock a non-existent enum member. | 2026-05-18 |
| C-70 | 1 | **`_resolve_evaluation_sequence_number("complete")` returned `None` — `TypeError` on `range(None)`.** `model.py:870` returned `None` for `eval_type="complete"`. All callers pass the result to `range()`, causing `TypeError: 'NoneType' cannot be interpreted as an integer`. Anyone running `--eval_type complete` got an immediate crash. Masked in tests by asserting `None` return. | Fixed: now raises `NotImplementedError` with a clear message. Test updated to expect `NotImplementedError`. | 2026-05-18 |
| C-73 | 2 | **`dataset_class()` returned `None` for unknown LOA — deferred crash.** `model.py:823-828` returned `None` when `loa` was not `"cm"` or `"pgm"`. The `None` propagated until it crashed with a cryptic error at a later call site. | Fixed: now raises `ValueError` with the invalid LOA and valid options listed. | 2026-05-18 |

Six `@patch` targets in `tests/test_modules/test_ensemble_check.py` updated to the canonical path. Boundary-test exemptions for `appwrite/file.py` and `logging/logging.py` removed. 1093 tests green. | 2026-04-11 |
| C-109 | **No ADR documents the eval-path Track B retirement decision.** Decision rationale existed only in `reports/track_b_retirement_views_pipeline_core.md`, not in `documentation/ADRs/`. | ADR-053 documents the eval-path Track B retirement decision, distinguishes it from the forecasting-path Track B (ADR-048), and cross-references the views-models companion spec. ADR-048 updated with cross-reference. | 2026-05-27 |
| C-97 | **DF ensemble timestamp divergence masked by fuzzy file matching.** All 8 timestamp derivation sites used `get_latest_model_artifact_path()` independently. | All 8 sites migrated to `resolve_artifact_path()`. Producer and consumer use the same method. Fuzzy matching no longer masks a divergence. | 2026-05-27 |
| C-99 | **PF save path ignores `--artifact_name` — uses latest artifact timestamp.** `model.py` eval and forecast paths always derived timestamp from latest artifact. | `resolve_artifact_path(run_type, artifact_name)` added to `ModelPathManager`. Save-path derivation respects `--artifact_name`. xfail test passes. ModelPathManager CIC updated. | 2026-05-27 |
| C-156 | **ADR-011 configuration table and "Mandatory vs Optional" subsection contained three false claims.** (1) Table listed `config_inputdata.py` — stale name, code uses `config_queryset.py`. (2) Subsection claimed `config_sweep.py` mandatory for all types — model-only. (3) Subsection omitted `config_partitions.py`. | Table restructured with Scope column (All/Models only/Ensembles only). `config_inputdata.py` → `config_queryset.py`. `config_partitions.py` added. Subsection rewritten: 4 base mandatory, 2 model-only, 1 ensemble-optional. Regression tests: `test_falsification_adr011_mandatory_configs.py` (4 passing). | 2026-06-05 |
| C-157 | **Dead `model_name` parameter in `template_config_modelset.py` with lying docstring.** `generate(script_path, model_name)` accepted `model_name: str` but the code string was a plain `"""` (not f-string) — `model_name` was never interpolated. The docstring falsely claimed it was "Included as a comment for reference." | Code string converted to f-string; `model_name` now embedded in generated docstring. Regression tests: `test_falsification_template_modelset_dead_param.py` (2 passing). | 2026-06-05 |
| C-140 | **TEMPORARY transform undo responsibility ambiguous per-model — silent double-undo or missing-undo corrupts predictions.** Originally Tier 2. `model.py` contained TEMPORARY blocks that could double-exponentiate or miss transform undos. | TEMPORARY blocks removed in commit `017c85a` (2026-03-17, E1/ForecastingStage extraction). `ForecastingStage` has zero transform code — the original double-undo/missing-undo scenario is impossible. Residual risk (cross-engine scale mixing in ensembles) tracked as C-158 with `output_scale` config key + `validate_output_scale_consistency()` ensemble check. | 2026-06-05 |
| C-160 | **Template defaults `output_scale: "log"` uncommented — can defeat C-158 validation.** `template_config_meta.py` generated `"output_scale": "log"` as an active default, masking scale mismatches. | `output_scale` commented out in template like peer fields (`targets`, `level`, `creator`), forcing conscious declaration. D-25 resolved as Ousterhout+Hickey+Martin approach. Template guard test added (`test_falsification_template_modelset_guard.py::test_template_config_meta_model_generates_output_scale_key`). | 2026-06-05 |
| C-161 | **No try/except around `spec.loader.exec_module()` in `validate_output_scale_consistency()`.** Config loading crashed with unguarded exception on syntax errors. | Added `try/except (AttributeError, ImportError, SyntaxError)` with `logger.error` identifying the broken model. Matches `_load_config()` pattern. | 2026-06-05 |
| C-162 | **No template guard test for `output_scale` key in model template.** No test verified the `output_scale` key in `template_config_meta.py`. | Regression test added: `test_falsification_template_modelset_guard.py::test_template_config_meta_model_generates_output_scale_key`. | 2026-06-05 |
| C-163 | **`validate_output_scale_consistency()` crashes if `get_meta_config()` returns `None`.** `check.py:259` called `meta.get("output_scale")` outside the try/except block. If a model's `get_meta_config()` returned `None` instead of a dict, this raised an unhandled `AttributeError`. Unrealistic under normal usage (template always returns dict) but a defensive gap. | Fixed by adding `isinstance(meta, dict)` guard before `meta.get()`. Regression test: `test_falsification_merge_safety_c140.py::test_f2_get_meta_config_returns_none_crashes`. | 2026-06-05 |
| C-31 | **Dual evaluation paths (PF and DF) functionally identical.** Both paths in `evaluation/stage.py` end with `evaluator.evaluate(ef=ef, legacy_compatibility=True)`, differing only in EvaluationFrame construction; no tracked DF-path retirement timeline. | Demoted to tech-debt backlog (maintenance burden, not a production failure scenario). Re-open if a future change must update both paths in lockstep. | 2026-06-28 |
| C-42 | **Missing CICs for key data-flow classes (partially resolved).** CICs written for `ViewsDataLoader`, `EvaluationStage`, `ForecastingStage`; remaining gaps were `AppWriteFileModule` and `WandBModule`. | Demoted to tech-debt backlog (documentation completeness, not operational risk). Re-open when either class's public interface next changes. | 2026-06-28 |
| C-79 | ID reserved (consolidated into adjacent entry during 2026-05-19 session) | N/A | 2026-05-31 |
| C-93 | **Double-underscore `__reconcile_pg_with_c` in `ensemble.py` prevented extraction.** Name mangling made the method inaccessible for extraction into a shared mixin. | Closed — superseded by D-13 (retire EnsembleManager → DataFrameEnsembleManager). Reconciliation now lives in views-frames behind the injected `Reconciler` port, not this mangled method; fixing naming on a condemned class is wasted effort. | 2026-06-28 |
| C-104 | **Star imports (`from .X import *`) in 4 `__init__.py` files prevented static analysis of the public API surface.** `exceptions/`, `files/`, `modules/validation/ensemble/`, `modules/wandb/`. | Fixed: all 4 replaced with explicit named re-exports (PR #173, commit 45cf2ab); 1,528 tests pass. | 2026-06-28 |
| C-205 | **PFE `concat` silently pooled heterogeneous sample counts.** `_aggregate_prediction_frames` validated rows+identifiers but not `sample_count`; `np.concatenate(axis=1)` accepted differing widths → silently unbalanced draw pool (GH #160). | Fixed: fail-loud `sample_count` guard added to the constituent validation loop (covers concat AND mean); regression tests `test_falsification_s1_readiness.py::test_pfe_{concat,mean}_rejects_heterogeneous_sample_counts` + homogeneous-pass case. | 2026-07-05 |
| C-208 | **S1 publish gate had no defined pass criterion ("metrics sane").** Gate decisions would have been post-hoc judgment calls. | Resolved: acceptance contract encoded as executable checks in `tests/test_s1_publish_gate.py` (pooled sample_count == Σ constituents; all-finite; MetricFrame per target; exit-0 + peak-RSS runner-recorded). Owner may amend criteria before the run. | 2026-07-05 |

## Stories (Domain Layer Extraction)

Ordered sequence of incremental PRs moving architecture from "PIPELINE" toward "CONFLICT FORECASTING SYSTEM." S-01 through S-04 are additive-only (no existing code modified). S-05 through S-07 wire domain types into existing code. S-08 is governance.

| ID | Title | Scope | Dependencies | Related Concerns |
|----|-------|-------|-------------|-----------------|
| S-01 | Introduce `SpatialLevel` value object | Small | None | C-38 |
| S-02 | Introduce `TemporalPartition` / `PartitionSet` | Small | None | C-17, C-38 |
| S-03 | Introduce `ForecastHorizon` value object | Small | None | C-38, C-39 |
| S-04 | Introduce `ReconciliationInvariants` | Small | None | C-38 |
| S-05 | Wire `SpatialLevel` into validation sniffers | Medium | S-01 | C-38 |
| S-06 | Wire `PartitionSet` + `ForecastHorizon` into config sniffer | Medium | S-02, S-03 | C-17, C-38 |
| S-07 | Consolidate step-mapping via `ForecastHorizon` | Large | S-03 | C-26, C-29, C-39 |
| S-08 | ADR-046 (Domain Layer) + CI import boundary test | Medium | S-01..S-04 | C-38 |

### Story Details

**S-01: SpatialLevel** — Create `domain/spatial.py`. Frozen enum with `CM` and `PGM` members carrying canonical index names (`("country_id", "month_id")` / `("priogrid_gid", "month_id")`). `from_str()` parser replaces bare string comparisons. Zero imports from outside `domain/`.

**S-02: TemporalPartition / PartitionSet** — Create `domain/temporal.py`. `TemporalPartition(start, end)` with `base_origin` property (`start - 1`). `PartitionSet(train, test)` validates non-overlap at construction (`train.end < test.start`). `validate_rolling_origin(time_steps, max_shift_count)` replaces inline arithmetic.

**S-03: ForecastHorizon** — Create `domain/horizon.py`. `ForecastHorizon(time_steps, stride, max_shift_count)`. Properties: `n_sequences` (`max_shift_count + 1`), `required_test_length` (`time_steps + max_shift_count`). Method: `build_step_mappings(base_origin, n_sequences)`. `default()` returns production values `(36, 1, 12)`.

**S-04: ReconciliationInvariants** — Create `domain/reconciliation.py`. Named mathematical constraints: `sum_tolerance=1e-2`, `zero_atol=1e-8`, `enforce_non_negativity=True`, `preserve_zeros=True`. `check_sum_constraint()`, `check_zero_preservation()`, `validate_all()` returns violation list.

**S-05: Wire SpatialLevel** — Replace `SUPPORTED_LEVELS = {"cm", "pgm"}` in `core_config_sniffer.py` and `EXPECTED_INDEX_NAMES` in `core_data_sniffer.py` with derivations from `SpatialLevel` enum.

**S-06: Wire Partition + Horizon** — Replace inline arithmetic in `core_config_sniffer._check_evaluation_contract()` with `PartitionSet`/`ForecastHorizon` construction and validation.

**S-07: Consolidate Step-Mapping** — Extract `resolve_base_origin()` to `domain/origin.py`. Replace duplicated step-mapping in `model.py:1715` and `stage.py:251` with `ForecastHorizon.build_step_mappings()`.

**S-08: Governance** — Write ADR-046 (Domain Layer). Add CI test that `domain/` imports only stdlib + numpy. Cross-reference stories from risk register entries.

### DoD per story type

**Additive stories (S-01..S-04):** New file in `domain/`, test file in `tests/test_domain/`, zero imports from outside `domain/`, no existing code modified, all tests pass.

**Wiring stories (S-05..S-07):** Existing code imports domain types, inline logic replaced with domain method calls, all existing tests pass without modification, no new behavior.

**Governance (S-08):** ADR exists, import boundary test passes, risk register cross-references complete.

---

## Process

Concerns are opened during:
- Expert reviews
- Tech debt audits
- Falsification audits
- Post-migration audits
- Repo assimilation

Concerns are closed when:
- The underlying issue is resolved (code change merged)
- The risk is formally accepted with documented rationale
- The concern is superseded by a different approach
